  | source app quits | none (host watches apps) | stop (the AirPlay sessions end, the chosen speakers stay chosen), notice (`AppDelegate.swift:386-394`; `AppLifecycle.swift:54-56`) |
  | output can't start | `attachOutput` throws | disable, notice per speaker (S69) |
  | output device lost | `onOutputGone(uid)` | keep enabled, re-add when back (S68) |
  | pipe has no reader / reader gone | none (writer retries) | none (S89, S92) |
  | AirPlay engine down, crashing or unsupported | lifecycle state | restart, give up, Retry (S98) |
  | AirPlay speaker dropped | engine output report | pairing rules, drop with notice (S104–S109) |

  Notices are capped at 4, newest first (S153).

- **S205** *Required* (AudioSync design; core tests "a failure after a caught panic says where it happened and never what it said", "… a panic in an input write …", "… the driver panicked says where the latest panic happened"; CI's panic probe). *Where the bug that stopped AudioSync's own sender is.* When a bug (a panic) stops the AirPlay sender, the failure event the host takes (`ASC_AIRPLAY_EVENT_FAILED`) says in its detail where the panic happened: `panic at <file>:<line>`, the source file from its crate's folder on (`airplay/src/sender/driver.rs`; the core's own files as `src/…`), with forward slashes and nothing above that folder, so no home folder or account name. The panic's message never goes there: it may hold anything, a receiver's reply included. A panic inside a call on the sender or its input is named exactly. When one of the sender's own threads ended instead (its driver, or discovery), no call caught anything, and the detail names the most recent panic in the process since that sender was created as `latest panic at <file>:<line>`, which, with one sender, is usually the one that ended it (a session thread may have recovered from it, so the wording doesn't claim more); with no panic since, a discovery that ended for another reason included, the detail is empty. The core notes the locations with a panic hook it installs once, when the first sender is created, and that hook then runs the one that was there before (the default hook still prints to standard error). Noting a location allocates only while a panic is under way, which allocates anyway (S126). The host writes the detail to its debug log with the failure (the Mac's `AUDIOSYNC_DEBUG` log) and never shows it to the user. The release build keeps unwinding, and CI checks that in the library the app links: it builds the same prelinked library with an extra entry that panics inside a sender call's guard (a build option the app's library never has), links it into a small C program and expects the call's failure value, the failure event with a location, and the program's normal exit.

## 16. Edge cases

- **S137** *Device unplug and replug.* *Observed* (S68). The renderer stops, `outputLost` fires, the UID stays enabled; on replug the reconcile that Core Audio's device-list notice brings (S101) re-adds it with its configured delay and gain. *Required:* the new reader anchors at `W − De` (S45), so it is in sync on its first callback, and nothing from before the unplug is replayed.
- **S138** *Bluetooth drop.* *Superseded in part by S175*: a Bluetooth device that sounds late gets a negative offset instead of "a little less delay". *Observed* (S68; `README.md`: Bluetooth adds its own lag, so users set a little less delay). Handled like an unplug. *Required:* a device that returns at a different nominal rate (e.g. a headset profile) is rendered through S62; its extra latency is not compensated automatically.
- **S139** *Sleep and wake.* *Retired in part with the bundled engine (2026-10-20)*: the Observed part (the output poll, the wake grace, pipe playback) describes that engine and is a record; the Required part still stands for the capture and the local outputs, all but its sentence on the FIFO feed, which went with it. *Observed* (`AirPlayReconcile.swift:140-191`; `AppDelegate+AirPlay.swift:9-20`, `:234-262`; tests "EC3 sleep: …", "EC3 wake: …"). The host follows the system's sleep and wake notices; the displays waking also counts as a wake while the host still takes the Mac to be asleep, in case the wake notice is missed. From going to sleep until waking it sends no output poll, and an answer or a failure that comes back meanwhile decides nothing and doesn't count toward reachability (S102). A wake polls at once and opens a wake grace of 20 s, counted from the engine's first answer after the wake, however long that takes; failed polls don't start it. During the grace a wanted `open` speaker the engine reports off is selected again instead of being dropped, at most once per poll (S103, S104), and pipe playback is requested again with each such selection (S99). A speaker the user switched off stays off, one that isn't `open` follows S106–S107, and a new engine run is a restart (S102, S143). After the grace the usual rules apply (S104, S106–S109): an `open` speaker still reported off is dropped as unreachable with a notice (S148). *Required:* nothing else needs dedicated handling, and nothing may crash: a callback gap of any length is a stall (S51, S55: resume at the exact delay, no replay); a capture format change after wake goes through S20; a device that vanished goes through S68; capture that never resumes surfaces through S18; the FIFO backlog cap (S90) keeps stale audio from reaching AirPlay, and a pipe the engine kept open but stopped reading across the sleep resumes at the live edge (S203), so the writer's own backlog adds no lag after the wake; what sat in the kernel's pipe buffer still plays first.
- **S140** *Sample-rate change.* *Observed* (S20, S30). A capture rate change restarts the session: new ring history at the new Rc, equalizer coefficients recomputed and state zeroed, FIFO resampler re-rated, renderers re-added. *Required:* an output device's rate change is absorbed by its renderer's converter (S62) with the reader position untouched; if the renderer can't reconfigure, it reports `outputLost` (S68).
- **S141** *Source switch.* *Required* (rewritten 2026-10-25; `AudioSource.swift`, `CaptureSwitch`; `AppDelegate+Engine.swift`, `startEngine(switching:)`; tests "EC5 source: a switch keeps the speakers connected and says how many", "EC5 source: a switch from a 44.1 kHz input to a 48 kHz app keeps the speakers at the new rate", "EC5 source: a new source that can't start gives way to the old one", "EC5 source: a denied microphone keeps the old source playing", "EC5 source: streaming stops only when the old source can't start again either", "EC5 source: a failed switch names the new source and the one still playing", "EC5 source: a failed channel pair switch names both pairs"). A source change while streaming, and a channel pair change of the input device captured (S14), rebuild only the capture: the local outputs fade out and are re-added on the new capture (S186), while the AirPlay and Cast speakers stay connected and the AirPlay timeline carries on (P143, rewritten 2026-10-26): the rebuild is an underrun, filled with silence on the shared timeline, the new capture's first frame follows it, and no AirPlay session gets a FLUSH, TEARDOWN, SETUP, pair-verify or `GET /info`, so the speakers hear the new source about as long after the old one as the rebuild takes rather than about L (1.75 s) later. The capture rate may change (an input at 44.1 kHz to an app at 48 kHz, for instance): the native input resamples it to 44.1 kHz and fades in from silence at the new rate (S189). The host marks the new capture's start on the native input, so its first block is placed as just arrived instead of dropped as late. A held E (S283) stays held across the switch, since changing it would flush every AirPlay speaker. Before 2026-10-26 the switch started the AirPlay timeline over, which flushed every speaker. Reason: a stop ends every AirPlay session, and the reconnect a few milliseconds later makes a speaker that asks for a code ask again, and some TVs time out on it. A new capture that can't start gives way to the old one: the old source or pair is stored again, its capture starts under the same speakers, and a "source" notice says "Couldn't start <new>, still on <old>" with the names the source picker shows (the pair names for a pair change). A switch to an input device that the microphone permission denies never stops the old capture: it plays on and the alert says where to allow the microphone. While macOS still asks for that permission the old source stays stored and plays on, so the source picker and the level meter show what is heard ("EC5 source: a switch to an input macOS still has to allow keeps the old source until the answer"); an allow then switches as above, a denial keeps the old source with the same alert, an answer that comes after the user picked a source or pair again changes nothing, even when the pick went back to the old source, and after streaming stopped an allow only stores the input ("EC5 source: an answer after the user picked another source changes nothing", "microphone: the answer runs the latest switch once"). The "source" notice goes when streaming stops or another source or pair is picked. Only when the old capture can't start again either does streaming stop, with the new choice stored and the start notice of S21. The debug log says how many AirPlay or Cast speakers stayed connected. The source app quitting with S227's Switch setting takes the same path to the whole system, except that a failed start has no old source to go back to. Before 2026-10-25 a switch stopped the session, every speaker included, and started a new one. *Windows* (2026-10-25; `desktop/src/audio/controller.rs`, `start` and `start_old_source`; `desktop/src/adapter.rs`, `switch_failed`; tests "a source that can't start gives way to the old one under the same AirPlay speaker", "a source that fails once the old outputs faded gives way under the same Cast speaker", "an input or channel pair that can't start gives way to the old one"): a source or channel pair change while streaming ends only the capture and the local outputs, which fade out (S186), and starts the new capture under the AirPlay and Google Cast speakers, which stay in the stream; the AirPlay timeline carries on as on the Mac (P143), since every Windows capture is resampled to the session rate, and the new capture's start is marked on the AirPlay feed when it leases it. A new capture that can't start, at once or once the old outputs have faded, gives way to the old source on its old pair, stored again, with the notice "Couldn't start <new>, still on <old>." naming both as the source picker does (a microphone that the Windows privacy settings block gets the notice that says where to allow it instead). The notice goes when another source or pair is picked or streaming stops. Only when the old capture can't start again either does streaming stop, with the new choice stored and the notice that the start or the capture failed. An old source that a failed switch gave way to and that then fails when it starts once the old outputs have faded stops streaming too, with the old choice stored and the notice that the capture stopped in place of the switch notice.
- **S163** *Source switch decision.* *Observed* (`SourceSwitch.decide` and `SourceSwitch.noticesCleared` in `AudioSource.swift`; `setSource` and `setInputPair` in `AppDelegate+Outputs.swift`; tests "EC5 source: …"). Choosing the source already in use changes nothing. Choosing another one while not streaming only stores it for the next start. Choosing another one while streaming, an input device included, rebuilds the capture as in S141, with the speakers kept on and the AirPlay speakers kept selected. Any real change clears the old source's "source" and "capture" notices. Picking the source or channel pair already in use while streaming clears only the "source" notice, which after a failed switch says "Couldn't start <new>, still on <old>" (S141): the pick answers it. While stopped such a pick clears nothing, since the notice says why streaming stopped. No pick clears a speaker's notice, a pairing or code notice included, or the AirPlay sender's.
- **S142** *Source app quits.* *Observed* (`AppDelegate+Engine.swift`, `sourceAppQuit`; `AppLifecycle.swift`, `sourceQuit`). With S227's setting at Stop, its default: When the app whose bundle ID is the source terminates during a session: stop, post a "source" notice (no modal alert). Stopping ends every AirPlay speaker's session, but the speakers the user chose stay chosen, and the source stays that app, so the next start plays to the same speakers from the same app (`AppDelegate.swift`, `appQuit`, and `AppDelegate+Engine.swift`, `stopEngine`; Windows: `desktop/src/audio/controller.rs`, `StopReason::SourceQuit`). Before 2026-10-21 this clause said every AirPlay speaker was deselected, which the bundled engine's host did and neither app has done since that engine went (2026-10-20).
- **S164** *Source app quit decision.* *Observed* (`AudioSource.swift`, `SourceQuit`; `AppLifecycle.swift`, `sourceQuit`; core `policy/source.rs`, `depends_on_app`; tests "EC6 app quit: …" and "mix quit: …"). Only an app source ends with a quit, and only when the quitting app's bundle ID equals the source's. Another app quitting, an app without a bundle ID, and any quit while the source is the whole system or an input device change nothing. A mix of several apps (S242) counts as one app source that ends only with the last of its apps: one of them quitting while others still run changes nothing, and the session keeps capturing the rest; "all except" outlives every app. The Mac judges a mix's quit one second after the app quit (`AppLifecycle.waitsForRecheck`, `mixQuitRecheck`), since macOS's list of running apps can miss the mix's other apps in the moment one of them quits; when the mix's capture finds none of its apps, the session is rebuilt after the same second and goes on with the apps still running, or fails as their quit (S227 e). An app source's capture looks at its apps again whenever macOS's list of running apps changes, not only when an audio process comes or goes (`AppTapFollow.followsRunningApps`), so an app that quits without an audio process, or that macOS still lists in the moment its last audio process ends, is seen too. An app counts as running while one of its processes still exists, read afresh at each look from macOS's list of running apps (its bundle ID in any letter case) and from the processes an earlier look found (`AppTapFollow.runningPIDs`): a running app without an audio process, a paused player for instance, is not a quit, and its capture waits for a new audio process, while an app that quits without a terminate notice, or that macOS still lists after it quit, counts as quit once its process is gone. A capture that loses its last audio process looks once more a second later, so an app that quits while playing is seen then; while the capture waits for an audio process it also looks every 5 seconds whether its apps still run (`AppLifecycle.waitRecheck`, `AppTapFollow.rechecksWhileWaiting`), so one that quits later, already without an audio process and without a terminate notice, is seen within about 6 seconds (that look and the second look a second later) and judged as on that notice. The re-check runs only while something waits: it ends when an audio process appears or the session stops or is rebuilt. A rebuild after a capture format change or a mix's loss also counts the processes the last session found, while a fresh start reads only macOS's list. A look that finds none of the apps running counts only once a second look, a second later, still finds none, since in the turn one app quits macOS's list can miss the others: an app of a mix quitting while another runs never loses the capture. The capture's own report of an app whose processes it saw have all ended (S227) needs no second look: it acts at the look that finds them gone, through the terminate notice's path, where a mix's app is still judged a second later. A quit matches the source's app regardless of letter case (`AppLifecycle.sourceSpelling`), a precaution in case the running app reports a spelling that differs from the saved one. Windows and Linux report a mix's quit only once every one of its apps has exited.
- **S227** *When the source app quits.* *Required* (AudioSync design, 2026-10-21; core tests "source quit: …"; the Windows app follows it, `desktop/src/audio/controller.rs`, `SourceQuitWatch`, tests "an app that quits gives way to the whole system …"; the Mac app asks the core through `asc_source_quit_decide`, `AppLifecycle.sourceQuit` and `AppDelegate.sourceAppQuit`, logic tests "source quit: …" and "EC6 app quit: …"). A setting chooses what a running session does when the app it captures quits: *Stop* (the default) or *Switch to whole-system audio*. It is stored as `stop` or `system`; a missing or unknown value reads as Stop, so a copy that never stored it, or an older copy reading a newer value, behaves as S142 says. Whether a quit concerns the session stays S164's decision: only an app source, and only its own app quitting (on Windows, where the capture itself reports that its app ended, the host passes the source's own app ID). With Stop the session does what S142 says: it stops and posts the "source" notice, the AirPlay speakers the user chose stay chosen, and the source stays that app, so the next start plays to the same speakers from it again. With Switch the session goes on with the whole system as its source, the way a source change does (S141, S163, S186): only the capture is rebuilt, so every output fades out on the app and in on the system audio while the AirPlay and Cast speakers stay connected, the stored source becomes the whole system (S12), and an informational "source" notice names the app that quit and says that whole-system audio plays now. When the whole system can't be captured, streaming stops with the start notice of S21, since there is no older source to go back to (Mac tests "EC6 app quit: switching to the whole system keeps the AirPlay and Cast speakers connected (S227)", "EC6 app quit: the switch to the whole system rebuilds only the capture and logs why (S227)", "EC6 app quit: a whole system that can't start stops streaming, with no app to go back to (S227)", "EC6 app quit: the switch to the whole system never waits on the microphone prompt (S227)"). Before 2026-10-25 the Mac's Switch stopped the session, every speaker included, and started a new one. Edge cases: (a) a quit while no session runs changes nothing under either setting, and the source stays the app; (b) while the source is already the whole system, or an input device, no quit concerns it (S164), so nothing stops and nothing switches; (c) the app starting again after a switch changes nothing: AudioSync stays on whole-system audio and never switches back by itself, since other apps may be playing by then, and only the user picking the app again makes it the source; (d) changing the setting while a session plays does nothing at that moment: the setting in force when the quit arrives decides, and changing it after a switch doesn't undo the switch; (e) a device change doesn't hold the decision back: an output device coming or going (S161) doesn't rebuild the session, so the quit is handled as in any running session, and a session being rebuilt when the quit arrives (after a capture format change, S20 and S140, or on Windows a capture starting again after Windows audio restarted or its device went away) counts as running: Stop stops it and drops the rebuild, Switch lets that one rebuild start with whole-system audio instead of the app, never a second rebuild after it; (f) the same quit reported again (the system's notice and the capture's own report, or a repeated notice) changes nothing the second time, since by then the session has stopped or the source is the whole system; (g) on the Mac a whole system whose capture fails is tried again, 3 attempts in all, 0.5 seconds apart, before streaming stops with the start notice (`AppLifecycle.SystemSwitch`; logic tests "source quit: …" marked S227 g): when an app's last audio process ends, its capture lets the app's tap go and waits for a new one, and a quit arriving moments later made the whole system's tap in the gap after that tap was destroyed, which Core Audio refused (`kAudioHardwareUnspecifiedError` after about a second; Podcasts on 2026-10-26, in 2 of 3 runs), while a start a moment later worked. So the first attempt waits until a second has passed since the capture last destroyed a tap, ending the capture that still waits for the app first (on the Mac sitting of 2026-10-26 a tap made 1 ms after the destroy failed in 5 of 5 runs and one made 1.9 s after worked); a destroy longer ago adds no wait. A start that ends before the capture (no speaker, a permission) is not tried again. A stop, a start or a source change by the user during a wait ends the attempts with no notice of the switch, a start that failed included, since its own alert has been shown; auto-connect starting the whole system during a wait ends them as a switch that started, with its notice. *Windows:* the setting is the check item File > Switch to All System Audio When the Source App Quits (on means Switch), kept in the settings file as `when_source_quits`; the notice reads "<app> quit, so AudioSync switched to All System Audio." With Switch, a capture of an app that quit before it could start (one waiting for the last session's fade, or a capture starting again after a loss) starts the whole system instead, which is the rebuild of (e); with Stop such a start ends streaming as before, with the notice that the capture stopped. *Mac:* the setting is the check item AudioSync > Switch to All System Audio When the Source App Quits in the app menu, next to Launch at Login, kept in the user defaults as `whenSourceQuits`; the notice reads the same. The quit reaches the Mac three ways, each through the same decision: macOS reporting an app that terminated, the capture finding its app gone (`.sourceLost(.appNotRunning)`, which stopped the session, so Switch starts the whole system), and the rebuild after a capture format change failing because the app is gone, which is the rebuild of (e); with Stop each ends as S142 says. macOS sends no terminate notice for some apps (Podcasts leaving a mix with Music, macOS 27.0.1, 2026-10-26), so the capture of an app source also reports each of its apps whose processes have all ended since its last look as that notice (`AppTapFollow.quitSince`, `SourceCapture.onAppQuit`): it takes the notice's path, a mix's app judged a second later, and the debug log says "app quit seen by the capture". The notice and the capture's report of the same quit, matched by bundle ID and a shared process, act once in either order (`AppLifecycle.QuitReports`; logic tests "source app quit: the capture reports an app whose processes all ended since its last look, never one it saw no process of", "source app quit: the capture's report and macOS's notice of the same quit act once, whichever comes first", "source app quit: an app quit seen by the capture takes the terminate notice's path, a mix's judged a second later").
- **S143** *AirPlay engine restart.* *Retired with the bundled engine and the FIFO feed (2026-10-20); kept as a record.* *Observed* (S98, S102, S105, S81, S169, "fifo: reader closing mid-stream does not kill the process", "EC7 engine restart: the reopened pipe starts as close to live as a fresh start"). The pipe reader disappears; the FIFO writer survives and reopens (S92) at the live edge, so AirPlay comes back with the latency it had before and the local delays still line up (S169); the host restarts the engine after 2 s within the crash-loop limits; while it is unreachable the AirPlay list and local delays stay as they were; when it answers again, wanted speakers are re-selected at their own volume, and each one the new engine plays by itself gets it back, at the first answer that lists it (S105); pipe playback is requested at once (S99). Local outputs keep playing throughout.
- **S144** *All speakers off, then on.* *Observed* (S118, S119). Switching the last speaker off stops the session and deselects AirPlay; switching any on starts a new session (ring reset, S39) with the saved per-speaker delays and volumes.
- **S145** *Default output change.* *Required; Observed* basis (S60; `README.md` "Using it": keep the macOS output on a local device). Renderers are bound to device UIDs, so changing the macOS default output never moves, stops or re-times them. System capture taps processes, not a device, and continues; if the change alters the capture format, S20 applies. macOS's own AirPlay output is hidden from the list by default (S151).
- **S146** *Six or more speakers.* *Required.* There is no fixed limit on outputs: each local output costs one reader and one renderer, all reading one ring without coordination (S58); the engine MUST sustain at least 16 local outputs at 192 kHz within S127. Every AirPlay speaker is fed by the single pipe stream (the engine fans it out). *Observed:* reconcile, pairing and coalesced volume writes are per speaker with no cap (S74, S104, S108); notices are capped at 4 (S153).
- **S165** *Many speakers, as tested.* *Retired in part with the bundled engine (2026-10-20)*: the local half (eight local speakers attached together) still stands; the engine's eight AirPlay speakers are a record. *Observed* (tests "EC10 many speakers: …"; `AirPlayReconcile.swift:257-273`; `SpeakerLayout.swift:67-85`). No host decision caps the number of speakers. One built-in output plus eight USB and eight AirPlay speakers are all listed: the built-in one under This Mac, the sixteen others in one connected list in name order, numbers in numeric order (S152). Eight wanted AirPlay speakers that appear in the same poll are all selected by that poll's reconcile, which returns one action per reported speaker in the engine's order (S104). Eight enabled local speakers whose devices are present are all attached by one output sync (S161).
- **S147** *Long names.* *Required; Observed* basis (S101, S149). The engine keys everything by device UID or engine output ID, never by display name; names are display-only, may be arbitrarily long Unicode, and never pass through fixed-size buffers or the audio path. IDs are percent-encoded in API paths.
- **S166** *Long names, as tested.* *Observed* (tests "EC11 long names: …"). Names are compared whole and never shortened: two 257-character AirPlay names that differ only in their last character sort correctly after a short one and come out unchanged (S152), and a 263-character engine output name with an emoji and non-ASCII letters is parsed unchanged (S100).
- **S148** *AirPlay network loss.* *Retired with the bundled engine and the FIFO feed (2026-10-20); kept as a record*: what AudioSync's own AirPlay sender does when a speaker drops off the network is in `docs/AIRPLAY_SPEC.md`, the local hold-backs follow S177, and S118 stops streaming once nothing is left enabled. *Superseded in part by S177*: when no chosen AirPlay speaker remains, the local hold-backs lose the AirPlay latency L instead of switching to 0. *Observed* (S102–S109, S78–S80). Speakers that lose the network are reported off; after the settle rules they are dropped as unreachable, removed from the enabled set, and noticed; if no enabled, listed AirPlay speaker remains, local delays switch to 0 live (S80); if nothing at all remains enabled, streaming stops (S118). Speakers that stop advertising leave the engine's list (`CHANGELOG.md` 0.4.0). An engine that doesn't answer polls is not a network loss (S81, S102). A pipe nobody reads is bounded by S90, and resumes at the live edge once it is read again (S169, S203).
- **S167** *AirPlay network loss, as tested.* *Retired in part with the bundled engine (2026-10-20)*: the engine's list and its reconcile are a record; the Local Network hint for an empty list still stands (S154). *Observed* (tests "EC12 network loss: …"; `AirPlayReconcile.swift:257-273`; `AppDelegate+AirPlay.swift:33-46`). A wanted speaker missing from the engine's list gets no reconcile action and stays enabled, while the speakers the engine still reports get theirs (S104). While it is missing it isn't a chosen AirPlay speaker, so local outputs stop waiting for it (S79, S80). When the engine lists it again it counts as appeared, since appearance is judged against the previous poll's list, and is selected again (S104). A listed, wanted, open speaker (S106) that the engine reports off with nothing pending is dropped as unreachable with a notice (S106, S109). An empty list while Local Network access is allowed never shows the permission hint, however long it stays empty (S154).

## 17. Host-side policies checked by the same suites

These run in the host application, not the audio engine core, but the logic suite checks them, so a port that replaces the host must keep them.

- **S149** *Observed* (tests "speaker key: …", "groups: JSON round-trip"; `core/src/policy/speakers.rs`, `SpeakerKey`). Speaker keys: `ap:<engine output id>` for AirPlay (`ap:native:<receiver ID>` under AudioSync's own sender on the Mac, S204, and `ap:<receiver ID>` on Windows), `lc:<device UID>` for local, `cc:<Cast device ID>` for Google Cast (S243); parsing splits at the first colon only (UIDs and IDs may contain colons); any other prefix, in any other case, is rejected. A stereo pair or home theater target is an AirPlay speaker whose ID keeps its `pair:<tsid>` or `theater:<group>` form (`ap:native:pair:<tsid>` on the Mac, `ap:pair:<tsid>` on Windows), and every key prints back as the text it was parsed from. A group (id, name, member keys) round-trips through JSON unchanged.
- **S243** *Observed* (`core/src/policy/speakers.rs`, `SpeakerKey::Cast`; `CastModel.swift`, `CastID`; `SpeakerGroups.swift`, `SpeakerKey`; `SpeakerNames.swift`, `castKeysMoved`; `desktop/src/adapter.rs`, `cast_key`; tests "speaker key: Cast …", "speaker names: Cast names …"). A Cast speaker or Cast group has the key `cc:<device ID>` on both hosts, the core's form; a device ID added by address keeps its `manual:` prefix and colons. On the Mac a Cast row's app ID `cast:<device ID>` maps to that key and back, and the key is what a renamed Cast speaker's name is kept by. A name the Mac kept before under `lc:cast:<device ID>` moves to `cc:<device ID>` once, at launch: a name already kept under the new key wins, the old entry goes, and a second launch changes nothing. A hidden Cast speaker stays listed under its app ID, as before. Groups and the auto-connect list still switch local and AirPlay speakers only: a Cast key in them is left out when the settings are read, on Windows, and skipped when a group is switched, on both hosts. Windows saves no settings version for this, since its Cast keys already had this form.
- **S150** *Observed* (tests "group name: …", "group rename: …"); *Required* for zones (S245; `core/src/policy/zones.rs`, `Zones::new_zone`, `Zones::rename`; tests "zone_names_follow_the_group_name_rules"). Group and zone names are trimmed; blank or whitespace-only is no name. A new group without a name gets "Group n", a new zone without one "Zone n", n being one more than the number of existing groups or zones, skipping names in use. A blank rename keeps the old name. A group that becomes a zone (S248) keeps its name as it is, "Group 2" included.
- **S245** *Required* (`core/src/policy/zones.rs`, `Zones`; `Zones.swift`, `SpeakerZone`; `desktop/src/settings.rs`, `SavedZone`; tests "zones: …" and the zone checks of the core and Windows maps). *One zone per speaker.* A zone is an ID, a name (S150), its member keys (S149) in order and a volume slider position from 0 to 100, which starts at 100. A speaker key is in at most one zone: moving it into a zone takes it out of the one that held it and puts it after the members, and stored zones that share a key keep it in the first zone only; a repeated zone ID keeps the first zone, and a stored volume is clamped to 0 to 100. Deleting a zone leaves its members in no zone and changes nothing else. A zone whose last member is removed or forgotten stays, empty, until it is deleted. A Cast group and one of its members are different keys, and a zone may hold Cast keys on both hosts. When a speaker's key changes (S204, or a Windows output listed under a new endpoint ID), the new key takes the old one's place, unless a zone holds the new key already, which keeps it.
- **S246** *Required* (`core/src/policy/zones.rs`, `click_plan`; `asc_zone_click_plan`; `desktop/src/adapter.rs`, `toggle_zone`). *The zone click plan.* Only present members count: listed now and not hidden. A stereo pair or home theater member (`pair:` or `theater:`) while the AirPlay 2 switch is off is not listed; it reads "Needs AirPlay 2 (Experimental)" and counts as absent. With S312's flag on, the AirPlay 2 path is on whatever the switch says, so such a member is listed as any other and never reads "Needs AirPlay 2 (Experimental)". The members a click switches on are the present ones, with at most one Cast member: the one already on, else the first in zone order the host can start now (not busy, such as one another app took over), and none while a Cast speaker outside the zone is on. A click never switches off a Cast speaker outside the zone; a Cast member left off counts toward neither on nor off and gets the reason (another Cast speaker plays, one Cast speaker at a time, or busy). When streaming is on and each member the click would switch on is on, the click switches every present member off; otherwise it switches them on and starts streaming when it is off. Every AirPlay 2 member switches on; all but the one P169 lets play (the one already playing, else the first, and none while an AirPlay 2 speaker outside the zone plays) show "Waiting for the other AirPlay 2 speaker" and count as on. A zone without a counted member changes nothing.
- **S247** *Required* (`core/src/policy/zones.rs`, `Member::in_target`, `Step::InTarget`; `desktop/src/adapter.rs`, `toggle_zone`). A zone key whose speaker plays as part of a stereo pair or home theater target shows "Plays as part of <target>", is skipped by zone clicks and counts toward neither on nor off; the zone menu offers "Use <target> Instead", and the key is never swapped for the target by itself.
- **S248** *Required* (`core/src/policy/zones.rs`, `zones_from_groups`, `migrate`; `asc_zone_migration_keeps`; `AppSettings.swift`, `SettingsStore.migrateZones`; `desktop/src/settings.rs`, `Settings::with_zones_migrated`). *Groups become zones once*, at the first launch without the migrated mark: in saved order, each zone with its group's ID, name and members and volume 100; a key an earlier zone holds is left out of the later one, so a group with no member of its own becomes an empty zone. The saved groups stay as they are, so an older Mac build still has them (an older Windows build refuses a format 3 file and starts from defaults), and until the zones are shown the groups work as before. On the Mac the zones are stored under `speakerZones` as JSON (`[{id, name, members, volume}]`) and the mark `speakerZonesMigrated` is written last, so a quit before the mark migrates again to the same result. Windows keeps `speaker_zones` and `speaker_zones_migrated` in settings format 3, written in one save; a format 2 file is read as format 3 with its groups migrating, and a fresh install has nothing to migrate. Once marked, later launches change nothing. S204 renames IDs inside zone members as inside group members.
- **S286** *Required* (`core/src/policy/zones.rs`, `Zones::remember_name`, `Zones::member_name`; `Zones.swift`, `SpeakerZone.memberNames`; `AppDelegate+Zones.swift`, `memberName`; `desktop/src/settings.rs`, `SavedZone::member_names`; `desktop/src/adapter.rs`, `absent_members`). *A zone member keeps its last name.* Each zone keeps, by member key, the name that member was last listed under (the device's own name, never the user's rename, which S243's names keep apart). The host updates it whenever the member is listed with a name that isn't blank, and saves it with the zones. A member that isn't listed now, after a relaunch too, shows the user's name for it, else the kept one, and only without either "Unknown device". The name moves with its key to another zone and to a new key (S204), and leaves with the key when the key leaves its zone; a kept name for a key that isn't a member is dropped on load. It extends S248's stored zone by one optional field, `memberNames` on the Mac and `member_names` on Windows, a map from key to name, left out while empty, so zones stored before it load unchanged and nothing else in the format changes. Reason: a zone shows its absent members by name, and the name lived only for the app run.
- **S249** *Required* (`core/src/volume.rs`, `zoned_output_gain`, `stage_level`; `asc_zone_output_gain`, `asc_airplay_set_zone_volume`, `asc_cast_zone_level`; `cast/src/status.rs`, `device_level`; `AppDelegate+Outputs.swift`, `setZoneVolume`; `desktop/src/audio/controller.rs`, `set_zone_volumes`). *Volume stages through the zone.* Each speaker has its own volume s, its zone's volume z (100 when it is in no zone) and the master m, all 0 to 100. A local output plays at `g(s) × g(z) × g(m)` (S71). An AirPlay speaker's level is `(s/100)(z/100)(m/100)`, spread evenly over −30 to 0 dB and muted at 0. A Cast device or group gets `SET_VOLUME` at m × z × s on 0 to 1 (C104). A zone volume change, and a speaker joining or leaving a zone, sends the playing members their new values at once, as a master change does (S72), and a member started later starts at them. Without zones every gain and level is bit-identical to the one before zones.
- **S250** *Required* (`core/src/volume.rs`, `own_position_for_level`; `core/src/ffi/airplay.rs`, `slider_position`; `cast/src/status.rs`, `own_level`, `start_own_level`; `desktop/src/audio/airplay.rs`, `slider_position`). *Read-back through the zone.* A level a speaker reports that the host didn't send moves its own volume to level ÷ (m × z): for AirPlay rounded to a whole step and clamped to 0 to 100, for Cast clamped to 0 to 1 (C104), and a Cast session's first status divides by m × z too (C105). With m × z at 0 the own volume stays as it is. A read-back never changes the zone's volume or the master.
- **S271** *Required* (`core/src/ffi/airplay.rs`, `read_volumes`, `volume_reported`, `VOLUME_TOLERANCE_DB`, `VOLUME_SETTLE`; `desktop/src/audio/airplay.rs`, `read_volumes`, `volume_reported`; `docs/AIRPLAY_SPEC.md` P122, P184). *An AirPlay speaker's reported volume, on both AirPlay paths.* The host asks each playing AirPlay speaker for its volume every 2 s, except one that scales its own samples (P121) and one whose reads stopped (P184's warning) or that refused a read on the AirPlay 1 path, which isn't asked again in that session; its next session (a reconnect, or the speaker stopping and playing again) is asked again. A reported level is ignored while the user drags that speaker's own volume slider, and, for 3 s after the host last sent that speaker a volume, when it lies within 0.5 dB of the level sent: that is the receiver rounding the host's own value (sent −26.949231, read back −27.000000). Any other reading is a level the user set on the speaker itself and moves that speaker's own volume only, as S250 says, by S249's curve backwards: the level is 1 + dB/30 (0 at −30 dB and below, and when muted), divided by m × z, rounded to a whole step and clamped to 0 to 100; a reading that rounds to the current position changes nothing, and with m × z at 0 the own volume stays. The master, the zone volume and every other speaker stay as they are. The host hands the new position to the sender as that speaker's level for a reconnect or a late member (P119, P180, P183); that hand-over doesn't restart the 3 s. *Source:* AudioSync design; a hardware trace of 2026-10-24 (a Samsung television with AirPlay 2 rounds a set level to its own 0.3 dB steps and moves 0.3 dB per remote press, so a press can lie within 0.5 dB of the last level sent).
- **S289** *Required* (`docs/AIRPLAY_SPEC.md` P116, P118, P122; S249). *An AirPlay speaker's device level.* The host shows and sets every AirPlay speaker on the speaker's own 0 to 100 scale, its *device level* D. The default mapping is the sender scale every AirPlay sender uses, linear over the wire range: D above 0 is sent as −30 + 0.3 × D dB, D = 0 as −144 (muted); a reading r is D = 100 × (r + 30) / 30, 0 when r is −30 or below or −144, and 100 at 0 dB. This is the mapping S249 already uses for (s/100)(z/100)(m/100), so without a model entry no level on the wire changes. How a receiver shows a dB value on its own display is its choice (an open receiver offers several volume profiles and a settable dB range), so the host keeps a per-model table, keyed by the model a receiver reports in discovery (P13) or `/info` (P41): for a model in it, a list of measured (display value, dB) points, used piecewise linear in both directions instead of the default. The table ships empty and is only a slot: a model enters it only when a measurement shows a bend away from the default line, never from an estimate. Check: on a receiver with a display, display 20 reads 20 in the app and app 20 reads 20 on the display, within 1 unit at 0, 20, 50, 80 and 100, and a change on either side shows on the other within about 1 s (S291). Measured on a Samsung television with AirPlay 2 only (2026-10-24, master 63): own volumes 0, 1, 50, 90 and 100 showed 0, 1, 31, 56 and 63 on the television, the default line rounded down, so that model needs no entry. Both hosts take the mapping and the table from the core, so the Mac and Windows show the same number. *Source:* public protocol write-ups (−30 to 0 dB, −144 muted, P116); observed behavior of open-source receivers and senders (an open media server maps its 0 to 100 slider to −30 to 0 dB and back in a straight line, and an open receiver's documentation says the AirPlay volume maps linearly onto its slider while its volume profiles and dB range are settable); hardware traces of 2026-10-24 and 2026-10-25 (the television above, its display read at five positions). *Required:* the default line and the measured-only table are AudioSync design.
- **S290** *Required* (S249, S250, S289; `core/src/volume.rs`, `device_level`, `row_move`, `own_for_device_level`, `level_set_on_speaker`; `asc_airplay_device_level`, `asc_airplay_row_move`, `asc_airplay_row_level`; shared vectors `core/tests/vectors/s290_volume.json`). *The row, the master and the stored volume: the master and the zone are multipliers, never a ceiling.* An AirPlay row shows the speaker's device level D rounded down to a whole number (with 0.01 of slack for arithmetic error), as the measured television shows it (S289), not its own volume s; the master stays a separate control. With k = (z/100)(m/100), z the zone volume (100 outside a zone) and m the master, D = min(100, s × k). s is stored unrounded and may pass 100, up to 100 / k at the k it was stored under. A row moved to R sends the speaker D = R exactly (S289) and stores s = R / k, so the row shows R at once and never jumps on release; a row move never changes the master (S250). With k at 0 the move sends nothing and s stays. A master or zone move keeps s and sends every playing member min(100, s × k) (S72, S249); the row then shows that D rounded down, and moving back gives the earlier level again, since s keeps what the cap at 100 hid (a row at 80 by the speaker's remote with the master at 63 reads 100 with the master at 100 and 80 again back at 63). A level the user set on the speaker itself (S291) sets D from the reading by S289, the row to D rounded down, and s to D / k, unclamped; with k at 0 it leaves s as it is and the row shows that D until s, the zone or the master moves or the host sends the speaker its level again (an add, the end of a calibration); the master, the zone volume and every other speaker stay as they are (S250). A remote's VolumeUp, VolumeDown and MuteToggle are row moves and its DeviceVolume a change made on the speaker (S275 to S277). Every path that changes a speaker's level goes through these core functions: the Mac and Windows hosts store s as it comes back, unrounded and not capped at 100, and load it so from their settings, presets and the speaker's add; both are tested against the shared vectors. This replaces the AirPlay part of S250 and the own-volume display of S271; Cast rows keep C104. *Source:* AudioSync design; maintainer, 2026-10-24 09.50.
- **S291** *Required* (`docs/AIRPLAY_SPEC.md` P120, P122, P183, P184; S271). *Reading the device level back.* While a speaker plays, the host asks it for its volume once a second, on both paths; not while stopped, never a speaker that scales its own samples (P121), and not again in a session whose reads stopped (P184). A change made on the speaker therefore shows on the row within about 1 s. A reading never jitters the row through the receiver rounding the host's own value: after the host sends a level, the first reading within 0.5 dB of it is the speaker's echo and becomes the session's *baseline*, however late it comes, and changes nothing; after that, a reading within 0.05 dB of the baseline changes nothing, and any other reading is a level the user set on the speaker, applied as S290 says, and becomes the new baseline. A single speaker never gets that level sent back, the sender only keeps it for the next session, since a receiver that keeps sets on a coarser grid than its remote steps would round it and undo the press (a target's members still get it, so they stay at one level). A reading more than 0.5 dB from a level just sent is that kind of change too. Readings are ignored while the user drags the row (S271), and the next send after the drag starts a new baseline. A volume SET_PARAMETER answered with an error leaves the row at the level sent: one receiver answers every volume set 500 and applies it (P183's warning and resend follow only when the next reading shows the level did not apply), and the next reading shows whether it applied. This replaces S271's 2 s cadence and 3 s window on the read path; the DACP DeviceVolume of S276 keeps its rule. *Source:* hardware traces (2026-10-24: a Samsung television with AirPlay 2 only answered every read and moved 0.3 dB per remote press; 2026-10-25: the same television answered reads on a 0.6 dB grid, so its echo of a sent level can lie 0.3 dB away, and answered volume sets 500 while applying them); AudioSync design for the baseline.
- **S273** *Required* (`docs/AIRPLAY_SPEC.md` P186 to P195). *Remote commands from AirPlay speakers.* The host takes the events of P193 off the AirPlay sender's event channel on the thread that handles its other sender events, never on an audio callback, and turns each into an action through one decision in the core that both hosts call, so the Mac and Windows can't differ. An event names a speaker or a target (P176) and one of PlayPause, Play, Pause, Next, Previous, VolumeUp, VolumeDown, MuteToggle or DeviceVolume with a level. An event for a speaker the host no longer plays (switched off, removed, or its session ended) does nothing. This version has no setting to turn remote commands off. AudioSync's own design.
- **S274** *Required.* *Transport commands go to the host's own now-playing control*, the same one the now-playing card's buttons use, for the app that control follows: on the Mac, the scripted play-pause, next-track and previous-track of the followed app; on Windows, the media session's controls; on Linux, the player's MPRIS methods where the host has them. PlayPause, Next and Previous go as they are. Play goes as PlayPause only when the latest now-playing reading says the app is paused, and Pause only when it says the app plays; with no reading they go as PlayPause, and when the app is already in that state nothing goes. With no app to control the command does nothing. The host never posts synthetic media-key events, which on the Mac would need the Accessibility permission. *Source:* `docs/AIRPLAY_SPEC.md` P192 (the commands); AudioSync design.
- **S292** *Required* (`docs/AIRPLAY_SPEC.md` P213; S274). *An explicit pause of the AirPlay speakers.* Two things pause the speakers (P213): a play-pause from a speaker's remote or the now-playing card's button, sent while the latest now-playing reading shows the app the control follows playing (play-pause reaches the player as a toggle, so the reading gives the direction; with the app read as paused, or with no reading, nothing is paused); and, while the speakers play the stream, the source player itself seen going from playing to paused, from one now-playing reading to the next (on the Mac each poll or refresh, on Windows each media-session reading), whatever paused it, the player's own button or a media key the system sent it. Under All System Audio that is the player the reading counts, so after a switch the new player's own pause counts the same way. A transition counts only when it is seen: the two readings name the same player and both know its state, so the first reading after the readings start, a reading after one with no player or an unknown state, and a switch to a player that was already paused are no transition; only the playing and paused states count, every other state (stopped, changing tracks, opening, fast forwarding, rewinding) is unknown, so a player that quits, stops, changes tracks or seeks never pauses; and a repeated paused reading does nothing. The host never takes media keys from the player and adds no key capture. Resume mirrors it: any play-pause while paused resumes, whatever the reading says, and so does the paused player seen going from paused to playing, at once. The host also resumes on its own, never pausing on its own: when a reading taken at least 3 s after the pause shows anything but that app paused (it plays, another app counts, or none does); when its capture carries sound again, counted only from 3 s after the pause and only once 200 ms in total of frames with a sample above -60 dBFS arrived, so digital silence and dither never count and a source the reading doesn't cover still plays; and at a new capture or when streaming stops. Silence never pauses. The 3 s cover readings and audio that may predate the app's own pause; they are longer than the receivers' buffer, so a resume after them is never heard as a cut. The 3 s count from the reading that saw the pause, which on the Mac comes up to one 2 s poll after the player paused, so sound in the first 3 to 5 s after the player's own pause may not resume. The debug log line of each resume names its cause: a play-pause (on Windows, the app button or a speaker remote), a player reading, sound heard, a new capture, or the readings or streaming stopping. The host measures its capture before the sender, which counts paused frames but never sends them.
- **S312** *Required* (`docs/AIRPLAY_SPEC.md` P2, P170, P214; `airplay/src/sender.rs`, `airplay2_auto`, `SenderConfig::with_airplay2_switch`; `core/src/ffi/airplay.rs`, `sender_config`; `desktop/src/audio/airplay.rs`, `SenderLink::start`). *AirPlay 2 per receiver, behind a flag.* A flag in the build, off by default, decides what the AirPlay 2 switch (P2) means; the environment variable `AUDIOSYNC_AIRPLAY2_AUTO` overrides it for one launch, `1` on and `0` off, any other value leaving the build's. With the flag off nothing changes: the switch (the stored setting or `AUDIOSYNC_AIRPLAY2=1`) turns the sender's AirPlay 2 path on, and the sender never prefers AirPlay 2. With the flag on, the AirPlay 2 path is on whatever the switch says. A receiver that only AirPlay 2 can serve (no `_raop._tcp`, a RAOP record the sender can't use, or an AirPlay 1 attempt that ended in Unsupported or Refused, P2), such as a HomePod, an Apple TV or a television that speaks only AirPlay 2, plays over AirPlay 2 with the switch off, and so do stereo pairs and home theater groups (S246). A receiver that speaks both stays on AirPlay 1 while the switch is off. The switch then means "Prefer AirPlay 2 for every speaker": on, it sets the sender's AirPlay 2 preference (P214), so a receiver that speaks both plays over AirPlay 2 too. The switch keeps its stored setting, its environment variable and its next-launch rule either way. *Source:* AudioSync design (advisor decision 2026-10-26).
- **S313** *Required* (S312; `desktop/ui/strings.slint`, `airplay2-prefer`; `desktop/src/view.rs`; on the Mac `UIStrings` and the AirPlay 2 setting's toggle; `SpeakerEvent::Path`; `core/src/ffi/airplay.rs`, `note_session_path`; `desktop/src/audio/airplay.rs`, `note_session_path`). *The switch's label under the flag.* With S312's flag on, the AirPlay 2 switch reads "Prefer AirPlay 2", through the strings file of each host (strings.slint on Windows, UIStrings on the Mac), and so do its note, the notice after it changes and the Guide; with the flag off it reads "AirPlay 2 (Experimental)" as before. A speaker's row names the path its session takes (P214): "AirPlay" while a session runs over AirPlay 1, "AirPlay 2" while one runs over AirPlay 2, so a receiver that speaks both and plays over AirPlay 1 while the AirPlay 2 place is taken reads "AirPlay". Without a session, or once it stopped (its sender stopping or Retry replacing it ends it too), the row names the path the preference would choose; while the AirPlay 2 place is held, that can read "AirPlay 2" until the next session reports its path. *Source:* AudioSync design (advisor decision 2026-10-26).
- **S275** *Required.* *VolumeUp and VolumeDown* are a row move (S290): from the row the speaker's latest device level D shows, including one the host hasn't sent yet, R = clamp(floor(D) ± 5, 0, 100); the speaker gets D = R and the own volume becomes R / k, unrounded. An up step at row 100 and a down step at row 0 change nothing, the own volume included. The result is handled as the end of a drag of that speaker's row: the host sends the speaker its new level, which starts S271's 3 s window. The zone volume, the master and every other speaker stay as they are (S250). *Source:* an open-source AirPlay sender moves a speaker 5 of 100 per DACP step; AudioSync design.
- **S276** *Required.* *DeviceVolume*, a level in dB the receiver asks for, is read as a reported level under S271's rules: ignored while the user drags that speaker's own slider, and ignored when it lies within 0.5 dB of the level the host sent that speaker in the last 3 s, as the receiver's echo of the host's own value; otherwise it is a change made on the speaker (S290): the row's device level becomes the level asked for by S289 (−144 and anything at or below −30 dB give 0) and the own volume D / k, unrounded and not capped at 100; with k at 0 the own volume stays and the row shows that level, as S290 holds a level set on the speaker. A level equal to the row's current device level changes nothing. Unlike a read-back, a level that moves the position is then sent back to the speaker as after a drag (S275), since a receiver that asks by DACP may wait for the sender to apply the change; the echo of that send falls in the 3 s window. *Source:* `docs/AIRPLAY_SPEC.md` P192; an open-source AirPlay sender applies the receiver's level and sends it to the device; AudioSync design.
- **S277** *Required.* *MuteToggle* moves that speaker's row to 0 (S290) and remembers the row it showed; a second MuteToggle while the level is still 0, in the same session, moves the row back to the remembered row, and with nothing remembered (the user moved the volume, or the session is new) it does nothing. Both changes are sent as in S275. AudioSync's own design.
- **S278** *Required.* *Targets and repeats.* A command from any member of a stereo pair or home theater group acts on the target's row and its one volume (P180). Commands of the same kind that arrive within 300 ms of each other count once when they are transport commands, whichever speakers sent them, and when they are VolumeUp, VolumeDown or MuteToggle from the members of one target: the members of a pair, or two receivers in a room, may each pass on one press. Repeated presses from one speaker more than 300 ms apart each count. AudioSync's own design.
- **S279** *Required.* *One log line per command.* For every event the host handles, the debug log gets one line: the speaker's name, the command (with the level for DeviceVolume) and what happened: sent to which app as which control, the level moved from which value to which, or ignored and why (echo, slider dragged, speaker not playing, no app to control, counted once with an earlier press). The crate logs the requests it refuses (P195), so no command gets two lines. *Source:* AudioSync design.
- **S251** *Required* (`core/src/policy/presets.rs`, `Preset::capture`, `Preset::sanitized`, `member_keeps`; `asc_preset_member_keeps`, `asc_preset_levels`; `Presets.swift`, `SpeakerPreset`; `desktop/src/settings.rs`, `SavedPreset`). *What a speaker preset holds.* An ID, a name (S254), the on set, each member's own volume slider position from 0 to 100, zone volumes, the master position and the curve. The on set is the speakers on at save, a waiting AirPlay 2 speaker included and the reference output left out, as speaker keys (S149, S243) in list order (S152). The zone volumes are one per zone a member was in at save, by zone ID (S245). The curve is the ten equalizer gains by value (S33). A preset never stores the preamp, the equalizer switch, a delay, an offset or the AirPlay adjustment. It holds at most one Cast key, and a fixed-volume Cast speaker (C68) has no own volume in it. On load, a key that isn't a speaker key with an ID, a repeated key, a Cast key after the first and a repeated zone ID are dropped; volumes are clamped to 0 to 100 (an own volume that isn't a number is none, a master that isn't one is 100), and the gains go on the controls' steps (S23). On the Mac the presets are stored under `speakerPresets` as JSON (`[{id, name, members: [{key, volume}], zones: [{id, volume}], master, gains}]`, a missing own volume as null, a preset without ten gains dropped); Windows keeps `speaker_presets` (`[{id, name, members: [{key, volume}], zones: [{id, volume}], master_volume, gains_db}]`) in settings format 3, at most 256 presets, the count a load keeps: at 256, Save as Preset… is disabled and a save is refused, so no preset is cut on the next launch.
- **S252** *Required* (`core/src/policy/presets.rs`, `recall_plan`, `recall_zone_volumes`; `asc_preset_recall_plan`; `desktop/src/adapter.rs`, `recall_preset`). *Recalling a preset is a full scene.* A stored member is present when a speaker with its key is listed now and not hidden. Every present stored member goes on, and every other speaker that is on goes off, Cast speakers included; streaming starts when it is off. The reference output, which the user can never switch off, is outside every on set and a recall leaves it alone. The stored Cast speaker goes on only when the host can start it now (not busy, such as one another app casts to), else it stays off with a reason; AirPlay 2 members follow P169: all go on, and all but the one already playing, else the first, wait. The master is set, each stored zone that still exists gets its volume (a deleted zone is skipped), each member that goes on gets its own volume, and a fixed-volume Cast speaker's volume is left alone. The ten gains are set; the preamp and the equalizer switch stay as they are. A recall never changes delays, offsets, zone membership, auto-connect marks or names. One notice (S153, one subject, so a repost replaces it) names every stored member left off by its shown name (S228) with its reason: not found, hidden, "Needs AirPlay 2 (Experimental)" (S246), "Plays as part of <target>" (S247) or busy; a recall that leaves no member off clears it. When no stored member can go on, nothing changes and only the notice is posted.
- **S253** *Required* (`core/src/policy/presets.rs`, `matches`, `Preset::is_current`, `current`; `asc_preset_matches`). *The current preset*, shown only on an exact match with no tolerance, against the preset as saved: the on set is equal (order ignored); every stored own volume is the speaker's now, except a fixed-volume Cast speaker's; every stored zone that still exists has its volume, and a deleted one is ignored; the master and the ten gains are equal. The preamp and the equalizer switch are not compared. Any change breaks the match: a switch, an own volume, a band, the master, a zone volume, or a volume read back from a speaker that differs. With two presets that match, the first in list order is current.
- **S254** *Required* (`core/src/policy/presets.rs`, `new_preset_name`, `renamed_preset`, `presets_from_groups`, `migrate`, `Preset::rename_key`; `Presets.swift`, `PresetMigration`; `AppSettings.swift`, `SettingsStore.migratePresets`; `desktop/src/settings.rs`, `Settings::with_presets_migrated`). *Preset names and the presets the groups become.* Names follow S150: trimmed; a new preset without a name gets "Preset n", n being one more than the number of presets, skipping names in use; a blank rename keeps the old name. At the first launch without the presets' own migrated mark, after S248, each saved group that S248 left a member out of becomes a preset with the group's ID and name, its full member list in group order (the first Cast key only), each member's own volume as saved (none for a Cast speaker, whose volume no setting kept before presets), the master and the gains as saved, and the zone volume of each zone a member is in then. Which groups lost a member comes from replaying S248 on the saved groups, so a quit before the mark gives the same presets; the mark (`speakerPresetsMigrated` on the Mac, written last; `speaker_presets_migrated` on Windows, in the same save) is set once, and later launches change nothing. A group that lost no member gives no preset. When a speaker's key changes (S204, or a Windows output listed under a new endpoint ID), the new key takes the old one's place in every preset, unless the preset holds the new key already, which keeps it.
- **S256** *Required* (`remote/src/state.rs`, `State`, `Zone`, `Preset`; `desktop/src/remote.rs`, `snapshot`, `calibrating`; `WebRemoteModel.swift`, `RemoteZoneRow`, `RemotePresetRow`, `RemoteSnapshot.calibrating`). *Zones, presets and the calibrating flag on the web remote.* The snapshot the page gets also lists the zones in order, each with its ID, name, volume 0 to 100, its members as the page's speaker IDs (only the members the page lists, in zone order) and three counts: members on, members listed, and members on that wait for an AirPlay 2 slot; a hidden or absent member counts nowhere. It lists the presets in order, each with its ID, name and whether it is current (S253, at most one). And it carries `calibrating`, true while any microphone run holds the outputs: one speaker's calibration (S232), Calibrate All (S235), Measure Again (S239) and a zone's run, also while a cut-off sweep plays out. With no zones or no presets the list is empty; the core refuses a snapshot whose zone member is not a listed speaker, is in two zones, or whose counts don't fit (waiting ≤ on ≤ listed).
- **S257** *Required* (`remote/src/server.rs`, `zone`, `preset`; `remote/src/state.rs`, `zone_change_from_body`, `preset_recall_from_body`; `desktop/src/remote.rs`, `actions`; `WebRemoteModel.swift`, `RemoteAction.plan`). *Zone and preset routes.* `POST /api/zones/<id>` takes `{"toggle": true}`, `{"volume": p}` or both, and `POST /api/presets/<id>` takes `{"recall": true}`; the IDs are percent-decoded, and any other method answers 405. A zone or preset the published snapshot doesn't list answers 404 (`unknown_zone`, `unknown_preset`); a toggle of a zone with no listed member the page may drive answers 409 `zone_unavailable`; a body that is not JSON or names nothing to do answers 400, a volume outside 0 to 100 answers 422, and a body over 4 KiB answers 413 before routing; otherwise 202. The host applies a volume first, then the toggle, as the zone's own controls do (S246, S249), and a recall as the window's (S252); a zone or preset gone by then changes nothing. A member that would ask for a code (a PIN or password) stays off when the phone turns its zone on or recalls its preset, counts as left off, and gets a notice on the computer saying to pair it there first, while the rest of the zone or preset plays; a click or recall on the computer itself is unchanged.
- **S258** *Required* (`remote/src/server.rs`, `ApiRoute::writes`; `remote/page/index.html`; `desktop/src/remote.rs`, `actions`; `WebRemote.swift`, `received`). *No changes from the phone while calibrating.* While the snapshot says `calibrating`, every write (a speaker, the master, a zone, a preset) answers 409 `calibrating` ("AudioSync is calibrating. Try again when it's done.") after the pairing check and before any body or range check; only the 4 KiB limit comes first. The host also refuses a command that arrives during a run, since the snapshot can lag behind its start. Pairing and unpairing stay open. The page shows the line and disables every control but Unpair, in both themes with text contrast of at least 4.5:1, and its policy carries the hashes of the page's script and style as served.
- **S287** *Required* (`remote/src/server.rs`, `Config::log_pairing_codes`, `ServerEvent::PairingCode`; `core/src/ffi/remote.rs`, `asc_remote_start`; `desktop/src/view/remote_window.rs`, `start_remote`; `WebRemoteModel.swift`, `RemoteStartConfig`). *The pairing code in the debug log.* Only while the debug log is on (`AUDIOSYNC_DEBUG=1`), each new pairing code adds one line to it: the first one at the remote's start, one per new code the user asks for, and the next one after a pairing used the last. The line reads `remote: pairing code <code> (debug only)`; the Mac's log puts its usual `[AudioSync] web remote: ` in front. With the debug log off the server never reports a code, so no other log, the standard output or a crash report carries one. The debug log then holds the live pairing code: anyone signed in as the same user (the file is private to that account: mode 0600 on the Mac and Linux, the user's own `%TEMP%` on Windows) can pair a browser while that code is valid, so debug mode is for development only. AudioSync's own design, so that a browser check of the page can pair from the log.
- **S288** *Required* (`desktop/src/remote.rs`, `debug_switch_port`; `desktop/src/view/remote_window.rs`, `restore_remote_with`; `WebRemoteModel.swift`, `RemoteDebugSwitch`; `WebRemote.swift`, `startIfEnabled`). *The web remote's debug switch.* With the debug log on and `AUDIOSYNC_WEB_REMOTE` set to exactly `1`, the web remote starts at launch for that run, on `AUDIOSYNC_WEB_REMOTE_PORT` when the port setting would take it (1024 to 65535) and on the saved port otherwise, and the debug log gets `remote: on for this run (debug switch)`. The saved on/off and port stay untouched, so the next launch without the switch is as before; once the user changes the setting by hand, the switch's port is dropped for the rest of the run. Without the debug log the variables are ignored. A remote the switch starts listens on the same addresses as one turned on by hand, so it is reachable on the local network and a browser still has to pair. AudioSync's own design, so that a browser check can run with no click in the window.
- **S259** *Required* (`MainWindow.swift`, `PresetsCard`; `Presets.swift`, `PresetRow`; `AppDelegate+Presets.swift`, `presetSnapshot`; `desktop/ui/main.slint`, `PresetsCard`). *The Presets card.* In the window's left column, after Master Volume and before Equalizer, a card titled "Presets" lists one row per preset in list order: the current mark (S264), the name on one line (cut short, the full name in its tooltip) and an options button (Rename…, Update to Current Settings, Delete). A click, Space or Return on a row recalls it (S265). Under the rows sits "Save as Preset…" (S262). Where the column doesn't scroll (the Mac), more than five rows scroll inside the card. The list and each row's current mark are published with every model push, an equalizer change included, since a band change can break a match (S253).
- **S260** *Required* (`MenuBar.swift`, `PresetPicker`; `Presets.swift`, `PresetPanel.showsRow`; tests "presets: no presets, the submenu holds No Presets and the panel row is hidden"). *The panel and flyout row.* Under the source picker of the menu bar panel (the tray flyout on Windows) a pull-down shows the current preset's name, else "Choose a Preset" in secondary text. Its menu lists every preset with a check on the current one; choosing one recalls it and the panel stays open. The row shows only while at least one preset exists and offers recall only: Save, Rename, Update and Delete stay in the window. The panel grows by the row while it shows.
- **S261** *Required* (`MainMenu.swift`, `PresetsMenu`; `Presets.swift`, `PresetMenu.entries`; tests "presets: Control-Command-1 to 9 go to the first nine in list order", "presets: a save appends, so the other shortcuts keep their keys"). *Menus and shortcuts.* The Mac's Window menu has a Presets submenu after Equalizer (Windows: View > Presets after Equalizer): every preset in list order, the first nine with Control-Command-1 to 9 (Ctrl+1 to Ctrl+9 on Windows), the current one checked, then a separator and "Save as Preset…". The submenu is rebuilt whenever the names or the order change, not when it opens, so the shortcuts work while it is closed and in every AudioSync window. A shortcut with no preset in its slot does nothing.
- **S262** *Required* (`AppDelegate+Presets.swift`, `promptSavePreset`, `promptRenamePreset`, `updatePreset`, `deletePreset`; `Presets.swift`, `PresetEdit`; tests "presets: a save with no speaker on saves nothing", "presets: a save appends, so the other shortcuts keep their keys", "presets: update keeps the ID, name and place", "presets: update is offered only while the preset isn't current", "presets: delete moves the later shortcuts up and changes nothing that plays"). *Save, Rename, Update and Delete.* Save as Preset… with no speaker on shows "Turn on the speakers you want in the preset first, then save it." and saves nothing; otherwise a name prompt with Save and Cancel opens, the name follows S254, the capture follows S251, and the new preset goes at the end of the list, so no other preset's shortcut moves, and is current at once (S253). Rename… prompts with the current name; a blank name keeps it (S254). Update to Current Settings captures what plays now into the preset, keeping its ID, name and place; it is disabled while that preset is current, and with no speaker on it shows the Save message. Delete removes the preset: the presets after it move up one shortcut, nothing that plays changes, and the preset notice clears when it named that preset. Update and Delete ask nothing, as for zones and curves. VoiceOver and Narrator say "<name> saved", "<name> updated" and "<name> deleted". The list is stored as S251 keeps it.
- **S263** *Required* (`MainWindow.swift`, `PresetsCard`; `Presets.swift`, `PresetMenu.entries`, `PresetPanel.showsRow`; tests "presets: no presets, the submenu holds No Presets and the panel row is hidden"). *Empty state.* With no preset the card shows its title, the line "No presets yet. Turn on the speakers you want, set the volumes and the curve, then save them as a preset." and Save as Preset…. The Presets submenu shows a disabled "No Presets", then Save as Preset…. The panel and flyout row is hidden (S260), and the shortcuts do nothing. Presets moved from groups (S254) count as presets.
- **S264** *Required* (`Presets.swift`, `PresetCore.current`, wrapping `asc_preset_matches`; tests "presets: the current mark follows a band change"). *The current-preset mark.* Which preset is current follows S253 and is worked out again on every model push, band changes included, so the mark shows and goes at once. It shows as a check before the name in the card (in the readable teal on the Mac, Tokens.teal-text on Windows), as the check in the panel or flyout menu and in the Presets submenu, and as the panel or flyout row's label. Color is never the only sign: the row's accessibility value is "Current". At most one preset is current, the first that matches.
- **S265** *Required* (`AppDelegate+Presets.swift`, `recallPreset(_:from:)`; `Presets.swift`, `PresetRecall`, `PresetNotice`; `Notices.swift`, `Notice.presets`; tests "presets: the left-off notice names each member with its reason", "presets: a full recall clears the notice", "presets: the notice goes once its preset is current or every member it names is on", "presets: with no member present nothing changes and only the notice posts"). *Recall and the left-off notice.* Every path (card row, panel or flyout, menu item, shortcut, phone) calls one entry point, which carries out S252's plan as a zone click does (members switched, AirPlay attempts begun for a recall made in the app, streaming started or the outputs synced, the Cast speaker synced, idle streaming stopped), then sets the master, the zone volumes, the own volumes and the ten gains, leaving the preamp and the equalizer switch alone. The notice has its own subject, "presets", titled "Some speakers stayed off", with the body "“<preset>” is on. Stayed off: <name> (<reason>), …."; when nothing changes, "Nothing changed: no speaker in “<preset>” can play now. <name> (<reason>), …." Names follow S228; the reasons are "Not found", "Hidden", "Needs AirPlay 2 (Experimental)", "Plays as part of <target>" with the target's shown name, and "In use by another app". A repost replaces the notice (S153) and a full recall clears it. The notice follows its cause: it clears once its preset is current again or every member it names is on, and a rename rewrites the preset's name in it. The Mac posts it as a notice only, never through an alert, and announces its text to VoiceOver; Windows reads it to Narrator.
- **S266** *Required* (`AppLifecycle.swift`, `StartOrigin.remote`; `Presets.swift`, `PresetRecallSource`; `AppDelegate+Engine.swift`, `startEngine`; tests "presets: a recall from the app or the phone shows no alert"). *A recall never takes the focus*, from the phone included. Whatever the path, a recall opens no window, alert or dialog and raises, activates or focuses nothing; it leaves the panel and the flyout as they are. On the Mac every recall, from the app or the phone, starts streaming with the remote origin (S120): it clears the start notices and lifts the holds as a user start does, but a failed start only posts a notice, so one recall reports through one notice. A recall from the phone (`from: .phone`) also begins no pairing attempt for the AirPlay speakers it switches on, so a code request becomes a notice too. On Windows a member the recall switched on (not one that was on already) that fails to start within the next 30 seconds (a local output that fails, an AirPlay speaker or a Cast speaker that can't play) adds the line "<name> (Couldn't start)" to the one preset notice instead of posting a notice of its own. Three keep their own notice: a Cast speaker another app took over, since that notice is what later recalls and zone clicks read as busy (S246, S252), and an AirPlay speaker that needs a password or PIN, needs AirPlay 2 or has no timing, since that notice says what to do or why. Once the user switches that speaker, by itself or in a zone, its failures post their own notices again. The remote command recalls and refreshes without showing the main window. An open window keeps its keyboard focus: a focused notice keeps it by its ID, a focused row keeps it because a recall adds and removes no rows, and the Mac's first responder stays where it was.
- **S267** *Required* (`MainWindow.swift`, `PresetsCard`; `MenuBar.swift`, `PresetPicker`; `desktop/ui/main.slint`). *Keyboard reach and visible focus.* With Full Keyboard Access (Tab on Windows), Tab reaches each row, its options button and Save as Preset…, after Master Volume and before Equalizer; Space or Return recalls; Control-Command-1 to 9 (Ctrl+1 to 9) work in every AudioSync window; the system focus ring stays on. A row's label is the preset's name, its value "Current" for the current one, its hint "Recalls this preset"; the options button is "Options for <name>". On Windows, F2 renames, the Menu key and Shift+F10 open a row's menu, and after a delete the focus goes to the next row, else the previous one, else Save as Preset…. In the panel or flyout, Tab reaches the row and Space or Return opens its menu.
- **S268** *Required* (`MainWindow.swift`, `MenuBar.swift`; `desktop/ui/`). *Both themes.* The preset controls take their colors from the existing brand tokens only, never a literal color, and their text reaches at least 4.5:1 in light and dark. The card with two presets (one current), the left-off notice and the empty state are shot in light and dark on both hosts.
- **S269** *Required* (`Localization.swift`, `UIStrings`; `desktop/ui/strings.slint`; tests "presets: the preset texts"). *User text.* Every word the preset controls show or speak comes from the host's strings: "Presets", "Save as Preset…", "Save Preset", "The speakers that are on, their volumes, the zone volumes, the master volume and the curve are saved. Delays and the preamp are not.", "Turn on the speakers you want in the preset first, then save it.", "Preset name", "Evening, Party…", "Preset n", "Rename…", "Rename Preset", "Update to Current Settings", "Delete", "No Presets", the empty line of S263, "Choose a Preset", "Current", "Recalls this preset", "<name> saved", "<name> updated", "<name> deleted", the notice texts of S265, "Hidden", "In use by another app", "Couldn't start" (Windows) and "AudioSync keeps 256 presets at most. Delete one to save another." (Windows, S251). Not found, Needs AirPlay 2 (Experimental), Plays as part of <target>, Rename's message, Save and Cancel are reused as they are.
- **S210** *Superseded by S246*: groups became zones, which have no on/off switch; a zone click counts only present members (S246). Formerly *Observed* in `WindowRules.swift` (`GroupSummary`) and three "groups: …" checks, all removed with groups. A group's switch counted only its present members, those listed then (local devices under S151, AirPlay receivers under S222): it showed on when every present member was on, off while any present member was off, and never on when no member was present. A member that wasn't listed counted neither way.
- **S204** *Required* (the maintainer's decision for the switch to AudioSync's own sender, the one change of saved IDs it may make; tests "speaker IDs: …"). *Saved speaker IDs after the switch to AudioSync's own sender.* An AirPlay speaker is saved by its ID alone (switched on: `enabledAirPlayIds`; its volume: `airPlayVolumeByID`; the `ap:` keys of groups and of auto-connect), never with its name or address. Both senders take their ID from the receiver's device ID, the MAC-style address in its announcement (docs/AIRPLAY_SPEC.md P8, P9): the bundled engine writes that 48-bit number in decimal, AudioSync's own sender writes 12 uppercase hex digits behind `native:` (P10). So when the native sender's discovery reports a receiver (found or changed) whose receiver ID is 12 hex digits, every saved ID that is the same number in the engine's form (decimal digits only, no sign, space or leading zero, 1 to 2^48 − 1) becomes `native:<receiver ID>`, as an ID and inside `ap:` keys, before the receiver's row appears, so auto-connect and a speaker that was on act on the new ID at once.
